# TODO

**Work that has been decided and not done.**

---

## What belongs here, and what does not

`vision.md`'s decision log has an **Open** list, and it had been carrying two
different kinds of thing at once — genuine questions nobody has answered
(*"Are the humors too neat?"*), and decided work nobody has built (*"`PC` →
`PORT` is decided and not swept"*). Those want different treatment, and mixing
them is why the second kind kept getting lost.

> **The Open log holds a question. This file holds a job.**
> An item moves from Open to here **the moment it stops being a decision**.

**Ids are permanent.** A finished ticket is struck through with the commit that
closed it, never deleted — the same convention the Open log already uses, and
for the same reason: the record of what was decided is worth more than a short
list.

**Add a ticket the moment a request would otherwise live only in a
conversation.** That is what this file is for.

### Claim a ticket before you start it

**Sessions run in parallel and cannot see each other's working trees.** Six
version numbers have now been taken twice because two branches both reached for
the next one, and a ticket is the same hazard with more of the day attached.

> **Mark the ticket `WIP — <branch>`, and get that mark onto `main` BEFORE
> doing the work.** An uncommitted claim is invisible, which makes it worse than
> no claim: it looks like diligence and prevents nothing.

*Clear the mark when the ticket closes.* **A stale `WIP` is the one failure mode
here** — if a branch is gone and the mark is still on, the ticket is free.

### Read the ticket, then check it — the oldest ones are the least true

**A ticket records what was true on the day it was written**, and this file has
been going long enough that the old ones have drifted. ***Four tickets closed on
2026-09-11 had a premise that was wrong, and in every case the ticket's own
claim was the thing that needed correcting rather than the code:***

| | it said | it was |
|---|---|---|
| **T-41** | 41 thin openings | *22 of them were `WRITE`, `FLIP` and `PUSH` — **which 2.8 settled on purpose.** The check was arguing with the design* |
| **T-26** | rewrite ~9,500 words | *a **read** of 9,500 and a rewrite of **18 blocks**, because vanilla already wrote its islanders in register* |
| **T-44** | two false positives | *two, plus **three holes the check had been silent about** — including 77 of 78 stale ability names* |
| **T-34** | the starter names are placeholders | *seven of nine were load-bearing; the other two were a **ten-day-old collision the newcomer caused***, which the git log settled and neither of us remembered correctly |

> **Spend the first twenty minutes proving the ticket, not doing it.** *Measure
> the thing it claims.* **If the number comes back different, that IS the work** —
> and it has been better work than the ticket every time so far.

*The corollary is cheap and worth saying:* **the git log is evidence and memory
is not.** *T-34 turned on which of two names was written first, and the answer
was one `git log -S` away.*

| ~~**T-164**~~ | ~~**Every overworld sprite and tile redrawn through the sprite server, in batches** (*asked 2026-09-18*). **The world art so far — buildings, ground, nature tiles, people, the player (T-52, T-61–T-85, T-105–T-129) — was generated in CODE by the `gba*`/`gen*` tools; the daemons are now drawn through ComfyUI on roverbyteseer (`spriteforge.py`, pixelbyte's model), and the world should be too.** ***The job, planned like T-131***: **a census of every overworld sheet and tileset (object events, trainer overworlds, primary and secondary tilesets, the ones already ours included), then batches in the order a player sees them, each drafted, on a contact sheet, approved, then built** — *tiles are a harder target than sprites: 8×8 cells, shared palettes per tileset, metatile layers and collision that must not move (T-125's zero-collision-change rule), and seams between tiles (engine.md trap 14).* ***Depends on T-133*** *for the daemon objects.* **First step: a trial on one building and one ground tileset, to learn whether the model can draw tileable 16×16 metatiles at all, before a plan is written.** ***TRIAL DONE 2026-09-19*** (*`gfx/drafts/t164/trial/`: `make.py`, `judge.py`, `sheet.png`, `restyle_strengths.png`*) — **the answer is split, and it decides the plan.** **(1) From words the model cannot author a tileset**: *asked for a house it draws a whole VIGNETTE (yard, trees, a border) and one of three in isometric perspective; asked for grass it draws hedgerows and crop rows. The best 16×16 grass crop does tile (seam ratio 0.09) but reads as a field, not ground.* **(2) Restyling the CURRENT art keeps the layout, and there is a sweet spot**: *at 0.35–0.50 it hands back the original with softer shading; at 0.65 a real improvement with the SAME footprint (warmer walls, wooden trim, roof and windows where they were); at 0.80 it redesigns — dormers, a door, windows moved — which would move collision.* **(3) Ground should stay generated in code**: *restyled at any strength, grass grows invented objects (flowers, fences, signs) and stops tiling.* ***So the plan***: **buildings get a restyle pass at ~0.6 from the code-drawn art, then are quantized back to their tileset's palettes and re-cut into 8×8 tiles with collision untouched; ground, paths, water and nature stay with the `gba*`/`gen*` tools; overworld PEOPLE and objects are sprites and go through the sprite recipe that drew the daemons.** ***Next step, a pilot***: *one real building end to end — restyle its metatiles as rendered, quantize to the tileset's own palettes (one palette per 8×8 tile), re-cut, check the tile budget and that no collision moved, build, and look at it in the game.* ***PILOT DONE 2026-09-19*** (engine `967863e4d`; `tools/gbarestyle.py`, `gfx/drafts/t164/pilot/`): **Doldrum's house, end to end, in the game.** *What the pilot found*: **(1) houses are a shared KIT** — *the left house's blocks and 27 tiles also build Doldrum's other houses, and the tileset had 33 free tile slots, so the restyle rewrites the kit's tiles IN PLACE and every house made of them changes together (a town that matches);* **(2) a palette row had to be freed** — *none was spare; row 7's four colours moved into row 10's unused slots, and the kit was redrawn in row 7;* **(3) the model SATURATES** — *Doldrum's muted roofs came back bright cyan and the pillars bright green, so `--keep-colour` keeps the restyle's LIGHTNESS and takes each pixel's hue and chroma from the house as it was.* **The result is modest and faithful**: *crisper roof ribs, a stone plinth along the wall base, the town's own colours; roofs a touch lighter.* ***Nothing structural moved*** — *block ids, collision, the door and the map are untouched; four ROMs build; seen in the game beside THE REPO.* ***The decision this leaves for the user***: **whether the gain is worth a pass over every town's kit** (each needs its own free row, and the model's saturation means `--keep-colour` is the default), **or whether T-164's effort goes to the overworld PEOPLE and objects, where the sprite recipe already works well.** ***PEOPLE AND OBJECTS TRIED 2026-09-19*** (*decided by the user: people and objects over buildings; `gfx/drafts/t164/people/`: `trial.py`, `judge.py`, `sheet.png`*): **the model has little to add at overworld scale.** **People**: *a whole 9-frame 16×32 sheet restyled at once keeps the walk cycle one character — but at 0.40 it hands back the original and at 0.55 it adds only speckle; a 16×32 frame has no room for the detail the model brings to a 64px daemon.* **Objects from words**: *hit or miss at 16×16 — the fossil (an ammonite) and the Lapras doll read; the signs keep a dark frame and the town map is a blob.* ***Conclusion, measured three ways (buildings, people, objects)***: **the sprite server's strength is the 64px battle sprite; at the overworld's 16px scale the `gba*`/`gen*` code tools, which drew the fable cast, remain the right instrument.** *What is actually left as vanilla on the overworld, by pixel against upstream AND checked for use*: **no people** — *CAMERAMAN, RICH BOY, SITTING BOY and STREAM's host are still vanilla's pixels, but no map places them and no script or code creates them (CAMERAMAN and RICH BOY are not even in the pointer table; RICH BOY lives on only as a trainer class, whose PORTRAIT is T-120's); the first report of "four people a player meets" matched pixels without checking use, and was wrong* — **and thirteen objects, every one of them shown** (*T-167*).~~ — ***Closed 2026-09-19, the question answered***: **the sprite server is the instrument for 64px battle sprites and not for the overworld's 16px world** — *measured on buildings (a faithful but modest kit restyle, which stays in Doldrum), people (whole-sheet restyles add only noise) and objects (hit or miss at 16×16). an empty slot takes the body's mid step*), **called after each load that can see a daemon**; *`PERSPECTIVE` copies the target's streaks because it copies the target's moves.* **`gbasprite.py` learns the four marker colours and writes 11–14 grey.** ***Not blocked on art***: *it can be built and proved on an existing sprite whose drawing has streak regions added — the first real test is a daemon in battle whose four moves are four different types, and the same species in the Index, blank.* **Icons cannot carry it (three shared palettes)** ***Built***: *`src/daemon_streaks.c` writes 11–14 from a daemon's moves at every load that can see one — battle (before either palette load), the ghost unveiled, `PERSPECTIVE` (the target's moves), move-animation copies, storage (party and box), hatching, trade, all five evolution loads, the item/TM scene, the summary, and field moves (found in the party by personality and OT); *the first flag counted any sprite drawing with 11–14 and found 346, every vanilla daemon, and it ran in report mode first.* ***The streak step is per BODY and MOVE type***: **light, or dark where the light one vanishes — 28 of 324 pairs** (*v11.210 corrected 9.4's "reads as a vein", which the preview disproved*). ***And a bug older than the streaks***: **`gbasprite.py` wrote each palette from the BACK, so DEADLOCK's badge was black in battle and two of PING's accents with it** — *one palette per species now.* ***Not yet seen in the game***: **no species has streak art, so today nothing on screen changes; | ~~**T-171**~~ | ~~**Is every daemon a thing that RUNS?** (*asked by the user 2026-09-20*). **The game is called DAEMONS, and a daemon is a process that runs in the background** — *`PING`, `FORK`, `THREAD`, `MUTEX`, `SEMAPHORE`, `SCHEDULER`, `TRACER`, `SENTINEL`, `ROOTKIT` and `SPAWN` all are.* ***But some of ours are a CONDITION a running system is in, not a thing that runs***: **`DEADLOCK`, `LIVELOCK`, `LATENCY`, `OVERFLOW`, `HEAP`, `STACK`, `FAULT`, `STATIC`, `RESIDUAL`** — *the user's own example is DEADLOCK.* **The user is explicit that this is not being proposed as a rule**: *the job is to MEASURE how many do not fit the mould, and only then to ask whether any should change.* ***What the review must not flatten*** — **8.2b already ruled three registers and they are deliberate**: *the ISLANDS' daemons are psychology (APATHY, DREAD, TRUST, HOPE, GRIEVANCE, VALENCE — Act 2 is mind), the legendaries are myth (ASCLEPIUS, ORPHEUS, PROMETHEUS), and MUSAI, ROVER and PIXELBYTE → REFORGE are the project's own.* **So the question is asked of KANTO's bestiary, which is the technical register, and the others are counted apart rather than judged.** ***The job***: **classify all 229 renamed species into (a) a thing that runs, (b) a thing a running system HAS or DOES, (c) a state it is IN, (d) psychology, (e) myth, (f) ours** — *a small tool, so the count can be re-run rather than re-argued* — **then a table of the Kanto names that are not (a) or (b), each with what it would become if it changed, and what that costs.** ***And the cost is real***: *a species rename sweeps prose (`port_vocab`), rewrites Index entries (8.2c), and 8.2b's own trap says **name tables are authored, prose is swept** — so a rename is never only a name.* ***COUNTED 2026-09-20*** (`tools/checkregister.py`, every judgement written down in it; a name missing from the table is reported, never guessed): **of 151 KANTO names — RUNS 31, DOES 16, HAS 42, STATE 19, WORLD 31**, *plus MYTH 3 and OURS 9 by 8.2b's ruling; beyond Kanto, MIND holds 26.* ***So 50 of 151 are not processes — but the two groups fail differently.*** **The 19 STATE names are still COMPUTING words** (*deadlock, livelock, latency, spaghetti, stampede*): *they fit the lexicon and not the stricter rule, and 8.2b's own ruling asks for the technical SURFACE rather than for processes — DEADLOCK and LIVELOCK are also a deliberate pair, which a rename would break.* **The 31 WORLD names split three ways**: *(a) logic and rhetoric — PROOF, THEOREM, CONJECTURE, REBUTTAL, CANON, RUBRIC, LEMMA MIND, LOCUS, VICE — which read as the LOGIC type's own register; (b) nature and weather — BLIGHT, WEED, SEEDLING, WILDFIRE, JETSTREAM, PULSAR, SLUDGE, FUMES, WISP, CRYOGEN — the ten genuinely outside the lexicon; (c) machines and creature-parts — BULLDOZER, RAMROD, NOZZLE, CHILLER, PINCER, CLAMPJAW, MITE, CAIRNLING, RELIC, REVENANT, HUNCH, TRANCE.* ***The recommendation put to the user***: **leave the states, treat (a) as deliberate, and rule on (b) — about ten names — and (c).** ***(b) RENAMED 2026-09-20, approved*** (engine `55ad1ed5d`): **WEED → SPRAWL, BLIGHT → SKEW, SLUDGE → DEADLETTER, FUMES → EXHAUST, WISP → DANGLING, WILDFIRE → OVERRUN, PULSAR → CRON, JETSTREAM → BACKPRESS, CRYOGEN → ARCHIVER, SEEDLING → CANARY.** *Each was checked against every name surface first — SNAPSHOT, RUNAWAY, ZOMBIE, ORPHAN and SPILLOVER were the first choices and all five are already an item, a move or an ability.* ***The Index entries needed nothing***: **they already describe the process rather than the creature** (*GRIMER's says nothing was thrown away, it was all kept somewhere nobody decided on — which IS a dead-letter queue*), *which is why the names could move at all.* ***What the rename cost***: **two lines of dialogue** (*SilphCo 5F names the KOFFING line, CINNABAR's gym names NINETALES*) **and twenty drawings renamed so `gbasprite.py` still finds them** — *179px and 159px, and 1722 lines in the game are already wider.* ***Honest about what it achieved***: *the ten left WORLD, but only CRON, ARCHIVER and CANARY became things that RUN; six are conditions, which is what those daemons are. Kanto WORLD 31 → 21.* ***SPAGHETTI RULED ON 2026-09-20 — KEPT*** (*the user asked after the rename; the entry was read before the ruling*): **it passes the test the ten failed** — *spaghetti code is a term of art, so the name is INSIDE the lexicon; it sits in the same bucket as DEADLOCK, which is already ruled to stay.* ***The reason to hesitate was that it reads as a JOKE first***, *and a laugh is louder than a wrong category — 8.2b files it under craft rule 6, the joke is allowed to sit underneath and is never pointed at.* ***The entry earns it***: **TANGELA's Index entry is `TANGLED` — "Every part of it reaches every other part. Nobody who could explain it is still here."** — *a plain description of tangled code and of legacy nobody can account for, which never mentions pasta; the second sentence is about the people who are gone.* **And the word appears NOWHERE else in the game** — *no dialogue, no sign, no other entry — so nothing in play points at it.* ***If it ever stops feeling earned the name is a one-line change and the entry needs nothing.*** ***Closes when*** *the count exists and the user has ruled on each name that does not fit.*~~ — ***RULED ON (a) AND (c), 2026-09-20*** (engine `14f096e70`; the user asked for the ruling): **(a) the logic and rhetoric names are KEPT — PROOF, THEOREM, LEMMA MIND, CONJECTURE, REBUTTAL, CANON, RUBRIC, LOCUS, and HUNCH with them.** *They are the LOGIC and CONTEXT types' OWN register (2.2: LOGIC is symbolic rules, brute force, proof), their entries are process descriptions, and HUNCH → INFERENCE → CONJECTURE is one line about reasoning — arriving at the answer without passing through the working, then stating what it cannot prove. | ~~**T-180**~~ | ~~**R in battle = PERSPECTIVE** (*held open by T-179, taken up by the user the same day*). **9.2's move is *become another thing for a while*; this is the same idea offered as a LOOK.**~~ — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-20*** (engine; `battle_controller_player.c`; 4.2, v11.227): **R on the action menu redraws the fight from where the opponent stands** — *their back where your daemon stands, your daemon's face where theirs was, both from art every species already has* — **and R or B puts it back.** ***The picture is the hook; the INFORMATION is the argument***: **the two windows become two accounts side by side — THEIR hit points TO THE NUMBER, YOURS only as a shape** (*untouched, marked, hurt, badly hurt, barely up*). *That is what each side actually knows, and nothing says so.* ***Decided with the user***: **it costs nothing** — *charging for a perspective would make a player ration the one thing this game keeps asking them to do* — **and it shows the fight and what each side knows, not what the opponent INTENDS**, *which would turn a perspective into an oracle.* ***Built INSIDE the battle*** (*the live sprites and healthboxes hidden, two pic sprites created opposite*), **because the Index's teardown would have meant rebuilding the whole battle to show a still of it.** ***Three engine facts it turned up***: **`CreatePicSprite` only assigns a sprite's `paletteNum` when the tag is `TAG_NONE`** — *handed a real tag it keeps whatever the shared template last had, which drew both daemons as black silhouettes; the slots are reserved through the palette manager now* — **`B_WIN_MSG` is not drawn at all while an action is being chosen** (*two attempts printed into nothing*), **and the prompt box is about eleven characters wide**, *so a sentence clipped to "ROVERCUB has 118 of".* | `engineGba/src/battle_controller_player.c` | 9.2, 4.2, T-179 |
| ~~**T-179**~~ | ~~**L and R do nothing, and the game has uses for them** (*asked by the user 2026-09-20, after the debug menu's own L/R were found dead*). ***What is already true***: **BUTTON MODE has three settings — `HELP`, `LR`, `L=A` — and vanilla gave the DEFAULT slot to the Help System**, *so L and R open a help tab everywhere instead of doing the paging `LR` mode already implements in the summary screen, the Index and the storage boxes.* **The useful behaviour shipped and was switched off.** ***Decided with the user***: **(1) `LR` becomes the default**, *so every menu gets paging immediately*; **(2) `L` = READ** — *the Index entry for whatever is on screen: the daemon in battle, in the party, or standing in front of you* — **which is the one the design wants**: *4.2 makes the Index the artifact that can only measure content, and a button that pulls the record up mid-fight makes that inadequacy present rather than three menus away;* **(3) `R` = AGAIN** — *repeat the last line, because FRLG has no dialogue history and a mistimed A loses a line permanently in a game this dense with writing;* **(4) the HELP System moves into the START menu as an entry**, *so nothing is lost and it stops owning two buttons.* ***Held as an open question, not part of this ticket***: **`R` held in battle = PERSPECTIVE** — *9.2's move is 'become another thing for a while', and holding R would show the fight from the opponent's side, their HP as they read it and your daemon's streaks from behind.* **It is the game's subject as a button and it is a second battle layout plus a rule about what it costs.** ***Closes when*** *LR is the default, L and R carry READ and AGAIN in the field, the party and battle, HELP is reachable from the menu, and the options text says what the buttons do.* ***THREE OF THE FOUR DONE 2026-09-20*** (engine, `start_menu.c`, `option_menu.c`, `help_system_util.c`, `field_message_box.c`, `field_control_avatar.c`, `overworld.c`, `stream.inc`): **LR is the default, HELP is a START menu entry, and R says the last line again** — *verified in play: R repeats "The ferry at ARDOR PORT is running.", twice; the HELP entry opens and returns to the menu.* ***Three things the work found that the ticket had not***: **(a) the start menu's DESCRIPTIONS were gated on HELP mode too** — *"A device that records DAEMON secrets upon meeting or binding" is our writing, and LR mode silently lost it; the condition is gone;* **(b) HELP stops being a BUTTON MODE at all** — *with the menu as the door, a mode whose whole content was "L and R open a help tab" would mean "L and R do nothing", so OPTIONS offers LR and L=A and a save still holding HELP is moved on when it continues AND when the start menu opens* (**the first migration sat on a callback the CONTINUE path does not take**, *which is why the save kept opening a help tab*); **(c) R = AGAIN had become DEBUG-ONLY by accident** — *its include, its string and its script each landed inside a `#if DAEMONS_DEBUG` block, so the two RELEASE ROMs would not compile.* **Found by building all four rather than the one being tested, which is the habit this project already has a trap written for.** ***STILL OPEN: `L` = READ***, *the Index entry for whatever is on screen — the biggest of the four, because the Index is its own overlay and calling it from a battle means saving and restoring that state.*~~ — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-20*** (engine; the party menu's `ReshowBattleScreenAfterMenu` is the real one;* **(e) the debug ENCOUNTER row now reads `163 POLL`**, *and its own promise of "L and R step by ten" works for the first time since it was written.* ***Still open, deliberately***: **`R` held in battle as PERSPECTIVE** (*9.2*) — *a second battle layout and a rule about what it costs, which is a decision rather than a convenience.* | `engineGba/src/start_menu.c`, `src/help_system.c`, `src/option_menu.c` | 4.2, 9.2 |
| ~~**T-178**~~ | ~~**28 of 75 portrait sources are drawn SMALLER than the sprite they become** (*found redrawing T-177's five, 2026-09-20, by `tools/prephires.py` reporting the figure's own drawn size*). ***The arithmetic nobody had done***: **the checkpoint draws on a fixed 8px grid, so a 512 canvas gives 64 ART PIXELS and the figure lands in about 50 of them** — *`sf_beauty.png` is 24x50, `sf_gamer.png` 24x49* — **which is then UPSCALED into a 64x64 sprite.** *The portraits that read are the hand drafts, whose figures are 270-290px and get DOWNSCALED: `portrait_juggler.png` is 161x289, `crush_girl` 292.* ***Downscaling averages, upscaling invents***, **and that is why T-177's outline improved every portrait but could not rescue BEAUTY's horns: they are two pixels in the source.** ***This is also a better measure than any T-177 tried*** (*AUC 0.80 against the same eye list, and unlike the others it names a cause that can be acted on*) — **and it is a property of the SOURCE, which is why measuring the built sprite could not find it.** ***The fix is the canvas***: **1024 gives 128 art pixels, so the figure is drawn at about twice the sprite and reduced into it** (*T-131 measured the cost at 82-106s a view against 23s at 512, which for a portrait is nothing*). ***Batch 1, the five T-177 named***: **BEAUTY, GAMER, POKEMON RANGER M and F, JUGGLER.** ***Then the rest of the 28, judged one at a time***: *a source at 50px is not automatically bad — SWIMMER F, YOUNGSTER and LEADER KOGA read perfectly well — so each is redrawn only where the redraw beats what is there, as T-169 kept 32 of 61.* ***Closes when*** *every portrait a player faces is drawn at or above the size it is shown at, or kept with a reason.*~~ — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-20*** (engine `5a7a8bd`, and the two batches after it; `gfx/drafts/t178/`): **14 of the 28 redrawn at 1024 and taken, 14 kept on purpose.** ***Taken***: *BEAUTY, JUGGLER, GAMER, RANGER M, RANGER F, CAMPER, CHANNELER, COOL TRAINER F, PICNICKER, COOL TRAINER M, STAFF SLATE, BURGLAR, PSYCHIC F, STAFF DOLDRUM SWIMMER, and TRELLIS (leader_erika).* ***Kept***: **BUG CATCHER, SAILOR, CUE BALL, ROCKET GRUNT M, STAFF CALLOW, STAFF BRAZEN, TAMER, LEADER BLAINE, SWIMMER F, SWIMMER M, YOUNGSTER, ELITE FOUR BRUNO, LEADER KOGA, STAFF DOLDRUM PICNICKER.** ***The rule the ticket was filed on held***: *a source drawn small is a reason to LOOK, not a reason to redraw — the last batch of eight produced exactly ONE take, because those were the ones that already read.* ***WHAT THE MODEL GETS WRONG, and this is worth more than the redraws***: **it drifts SPECIES** — *batch 1 drew a FOX for the jackal and both martens (and the fox is the CLEARS' animal), batch 3 drew a RODENT for the hyena and a FROG for the salamander, which would have put KOGA's species on a second leader* — **and it DROPS CLOTHES**, *returning a plain seagull in a cap and a plain penguin with no suit, where the suit is the joke.* **Both are standing negatives now, and a species is described by its FEATURES rather than named**: *the jackal's black-and-silver saddle and black-tipped tail, the marten's low body and cream throat bib, the bowerbird's short stout beak.* ***Three kept for reasons that are not about resolution at all***: **BRUNO's redraw put him in BLACK BELT's own gi** (*they are both kangaroos; the clothes are what separate them*), **STAFF BRAZEN's put a tarsier in a top hat**, *and CUE BALL's was crisper but bright cartoon green on a class whose whole read is a tough one.* ***The canvas rule is in 9.4*** (*v11.225*): **draw it at twice the size it is shown** — *1024 gives 128 art pixels where 512 gives 64, and the figure lands in about fifty of them.* ***And the check is in the tools***: *`prephires.py` reports a source's own drawn size, which is how the 28 were found.* | `gfx/characters/`, `tools/gbachar.py`, `tools/spriteforge.py` | T-177, T-169, T-131, 9.4 |

`tools/gbaoutline.py`, and the same pass inside `gbachar.index`): **the ticket's first job was the MEASURE, and the measure's answer was NO.** ***Nine statistics against a list of broken portraits made by eye over all 72 that are ours***: *crumbs (share of the figure in same-colour blobs of 1-3 px), same-colour blobs per 100 px, colour churn, rare colours, thin structures, fringe, loose pieces, edge colours, outline share.* ***What they actually found***: **crumbs and churn rank a TWEED JACKET and a JUDO GI worst** — *they measure texture, not breakage* — **and loose pieces finds the portraits with TWO FIGURES in them** (*TWINS, SIS AND BRO, COOL COUPLE*). *The best reached AUC 0.93 and still named clean pictures at its extreme, so as a list of files to clean it would have cleaned the wrong half — which is exactly what this ticket was filed to avoid.* ***The honest reading***: **the eye was judging whether the SUBJECT is legible, and that is not a property of the pixels taken one at a time.** ***But the work found the CAUSE, which is better than a ranking.*** **The pictures that read draw 65-74% of their silhouette edge in their four darkest colours and never the bright ones; the ones that do not draw their edge in EVERY colour they have** — *100% of the palette, on seven of the nine.* **A drawing resized with LANCZOS has a blended edge, and a fifteen-colour adaptive fit spends its slots on that blend**: *BEAUTY holds five near-identical blues from one dress and has none left for her horns or her hands.* ***The quantizer cannot draw an outline, because an outline is a decision about what matters and it is only counting.*** ***So the fix is not cleaning files*** (`cleandraft.py` was never run): **every silhouette pixel takes the darkest colour its own picture already uses.** *Nothing added to a palette, no colour invented.* ***84 pictures, all of them ours***: **the 72 trainer portraits, the four hearsay portraits, the four intro pictures and the four back pics** — *the user's call was all of them, including the intro, where the white lab coats had no edge at all against a light background.* **Edge-in-its-darkest-four, averaged: 38% → 100%, and running it again redraws nothing.** *A back pic is a STRIP of 64x64 frames, so each frame is outlined on its own — outlining the strip whole would read one frame's feet against the next frame's head as silhouette.* ***Excluded on measurement rather than taste***: **the overworld**, *where an outline at 16x32 is most of the sprite (T-170).* ***What an outline cannot do***: **BEAUTY is improved and still fragmented** — *her horns and hands were lost in the drawing, not at the edge, and she is a REDRAW rather than a repair.* | `tools/gbaoutline.py`, `tools/gbachar.py`, `engineGba/graphics/trainers/` | T-174, T-169, 9.4 |

These three jobs need `hue=False`, which keys on the flat corners alone.* ***The recipe for the rest of the ticket***: *source = the in-ROM picture on white; denoise 0.4-0.45; `hue=False`; judge at 64x96, which is where the speckle shows.* **Nothing is written: the tool was reverted and the engine tree is clean.** ***SEEDS DRAWN 2026-09-20*** (`gfx/drafts/t174/seeds.png`, `intuition_round2.png`; still nothing written): **four seeds each at 0.45, and a second round for INTUITION at 0.35.** *CRYSTAL: seed 1917 is the one — calm muzzle, the coat stays white, the purple reads; 7 tints the coat lilac and 42 and 99 dissolve it. both are back to their drawings** (engine `49875fe36`). ***The user's diagnosis was right and the fault was mine***: *I fed the model the 64x96 ROM PICTURE upscaled — blocky input — so it invented detail. `gbachar.py` already downsamples with LANCZOS; | ~~**T-175**~~ | ~~**The footprint in every Index entry is vanilla's** (*spotted by the user in play, 2026-09-20 — "some symbol or ink smudge" in the middle of the entry*). **It is the FOOTPRINT**: *Gen 3 prints a 16x16 1bpp mark between HT and WT, `graphics/pokemon/<species>/footprint.png`, and all 386 are still vanilla's paw prints.* ***Why it reads as a smudge***: **a paw print is a BODY** — *the one place in the Index where the fable's own rule is broken, on a screen whose every other line is a process.* ***The opportunity***: *a footprint is one bit per pixel and tiny, so it can be GENERATED — a mark derived from each daemon's own silhouette, or a per-type mark (the chart is the argument, and a type mark would say so).* ***Decide first***: **silhouette, type mark, or nothing at all** (*blanking it is legal: vanilla ships blanks for the unused slots*).~~ — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-20*** (engine `108b4b286`; `tools/genfootprints.py`): **the decision is the TYPE MARK**, *and the argument for it is what the entry page does not print* — **species, category, height and weight, and never the type**, *which Gen 3 keeps on the summary screen.* **So the slot carries the one fact the page lacks, and the chart is the argument** (invariant 3). ***The silhouette was the alternative and was ruled out on the format***: *sixteen pixels in ONE BIT is mush, and it would say nothing the picture beside it does not already say.* ***Eighteen marks, each its type's own 2.2 clause drawn flat***: **CONTENT** a token whole, **LOGIC** a turnstile, **VECTOR** an arrow, **CORRUPT** a blot spreading, **STRATUM** layers, **LEGACY** a cairn, **SWARM** many apart, **LATENT** a dashed box, **HARDENED** a plate, **ORACLE** a closed box that answers, **ENTROPY** a rising jag, **FLOW** a gradient descending, **GROWTH** a sprout, **SIGNAL** a carrier, **CONTEXT** two brackets framing, **FROZEN** a crystal, **EMERGENT** a spiral, **OPAQUE** filled solid. ***386 footprints written from `species_info.h`***, *so a retype changes the mark and the two cannot drift.* ***Verified in the ROM rather than by eye***: **eighteen distinct 32-byte blobs across every built footprint, so no vanilla paw survives**, *and all four ROMs exit 0.* ***One trap the tool hit and now documents***: **`[SPECIES_NONE] = {0},` is written on ONE LINE**, *so a non-greedy braced-block match runs straight through it and swallows BULBASAUR's entry with it* — **the parser splits on the label instead.** ***And one mark is carried by nobody***: **ORACLE** — *no daemon holds MYSTERY first, so the chart is complete and the bestiary is not.* | `engineGba/graphics/pokemon/*/footprint.png` | 4.2, 9.4 |

| ~~**T-135**~~ | ~~**The animation rule — what a routine's animation may show**~~ — ***Decided 2026-09-17: vision.md 9.24, v11.213***; *RESTORE released as its exemplar (engine `382bda0af`)*. (*T-134, wave 0; a decision to draft, not a job to start*). **Written as a 9.x section and approved before any family is touched**: *what an animation depicts (the process the routine names, not the creature's body doing something), where colour may appear (9.4 and PERSPECTIVE's precedent: colour where it means something, grey as the ground), whether a routine's own TYPE colour tints its effects, what the streaks do during an animation, and the budget — script-only (vanilla's effect sprites, re-sequenced and re-tinted) against new effect graphics.* ***Drafted on three exemplars before it is written as a rule***: **RESTORE, PERSPECTIVE (already ours) and one CONTENT attack** — *shown in the T-136 theatre, approved on screen.* ***Drafted and APPROVED 2026-09-17*** (*`gfx/drafts/animations/t135_exemplars.png`*): **the proposed rule** — *(1) an animation shows the PROCESS its routine names, never a creature exerting its body or gathering energy from outside; (2) the daemon's own palette is the medium, and grey is the ground: losing something is drawn as draining toward grey, getting it back as colour returning — which costs invariant 5 nothing (8.6); (3) the only colour an effect may add is a TYPE's — the routine's own, from `TYPE_COLOR` — never vanilla's decorative yellows, pinks and blue stars; (4) the streaks are part of the palette, so whatever happens to a daemon's colour happens to what it knows; (5) in Halftone the frame's greying wins (8.6a) and PERSPECTIVE's flash stays the one exception; (6) script first — re-sequencing and re-tinting vanilla's tasks and sprites — and a new effect graphic only when no existing one can mean the process (T-137).* ***The exemplars***: **RESTORE redrawn (debug ROMs only, `.if DAEMONS_DEBUG`): its colour drains to grey and returns in three ticked steps — a checkpoint reloading**; *PERSPECTIVE already obeys (1), (2) and (4)*; **WRITE (vanilla TACKLE) is the open one** — *a lunge and a shake is a body doing something, and under (1) and (3) a write would land on the target as a brief flash of the attacker's type colour rather than a shove; not built until the rule is approved.* | `docs/vision.md` | **9.4**, T-134, T-136 |

| ~~**T-130**~~ | ~~**Two Index entries were never written: WORM and MIME**~~ — ***Done 2026-09-17*** (engine `a6e482ff9`) (*found 2026-09-17 by T-47's read*). **Both are vanilla prose in both editions, and the Game Boy source never wrote them either** — *so the port carried nothing, and nobody noticed because each had been touched once.* **WORM** (*Ekans slot, `PROPAGATE`, on Route 4*) *is vanilla's LeafGreen line with `port_names.py`'s substitution run through it — "Eats the eggs of birds, such as PACKET and PING, whole", with no full stop* — **in BOTH editions, so FireRed shows LeafGreen's vanilla.** **MIME** (*Mr. Mime slot, `IMITATIVE`, MATTE's lead*) *is vanilla word for word in FireRed — "adept at conning people" — and vanilla with a move renamed in LeafGreen, "will suddenly REPEAT the offender".* **Both are GAMUT species, so each wants machinery as its subject and the mind left unsaid, CONTENT saying what it is and CONTEXT what somebody made of it, as SUBSTRATE's pair does.** *New Index prose is writing and wants its wording approved before it goes in* ***Written, with the wording approved first***: **WORM** — *CONTENT "It copies itself into anything that will run it, and leaves from there. It does not need to be carried. It does not stop." CONTEXT "Nobody sent it. Everyone who received it passed it on. It is counted by where it has been, and it has been everywhere."* **MIME** — *CONTENT "It mimes a wall with its hands, and things stop at the wall. There is no wall. Nothing that stopped has checked." CONTEXT "It copies what blocks the way, down to how the hands are held. Everything that meets the copy treats it as the thing."* ***A character count is not a width***: **at forty-two characters MIME's CONTEXT line measured 239px against the 234px Index line vanilla demonstrates, and at that width the approved wording needed four lines** — *so the text is wrapped by pixels, and one word was trimmed ("whatever" → "what"), with approval.* **The widest Index line is 234px in both editions; each text is in its own edition's two ROMs and not the other's, and the vanilla lines are gone from all four.** *The Game Boy build is a reference and not updated further, so its `dex_text.asm` keeps vanilla's.* | `src/data/pokemon/pokedex_text_fr.h`, `_lg.h` | **0.6**, **4.25**, T-47 |

Its error was measured on vanilla's saturated colours, and this figure is greys and browns, whose nearest saturated neighbours lend them their hue.* **The one-tint mix cannot turn a grey into a colour, drifts 6°, and scores the same as a line.** *The tool finds stale reflections itself — a base that differs from upstream with a reflection that does not — so a palette redrawn later cannot quietly repeat this; `player.pal` was the only one.* | `graphics/object_events/palettes/player_reflection.pal`, `tools/gbaplayer.py` | **9.10**, T-52 |

approved 2026-09-16***: *the frame, in commerce* — **bronze where the building asserts itself, verdigris ONLY where hands and time reach, green only on what is alive** — *the store is the thing that went up (craft rule 2's kid outside is right), the hideout is an office under a floor Corpus already owns (the sign reads CORPUS GAME CORNER), and the hotel keeps no daemon fixture because it is "a hotel for people".* ***Done 2026-09-16 — FOUR of the five buildings, 13 rooms, 0 collision changes across every one of them***: `gTileset_VerdigrisTable` *(the restaurant and hotel, 40 blocks / 105 tiles)*, `_VerdigrisStore` *(six floors, 57 / 100)*, `_VerdigrisBlock` *(four floors, 41 / 72)* and `_VerdigrisFloor` *(three rooms, 47 / 86, with all nine script-pinned ids reserved so the hideout stair still opens)*. ***It took three drafts and each failure was specific***: *(1) classifying `behaviour == 0` as wall swallowed every table, machine bank and the school's long table — **wall is what a border flood-fill reaches through blocked, ROLE-LESS cells**, and 98 distinct vanilla blocks came back as 5; (2) drawing per cell rather than per **cluster** — a kitchen is one 5×7 block holding a counter, a stove, a dresser and a plate; (3) structure without **surface** — vanilla spends 16–25% of every floor cell on its pattern and the draft spent 0.8%, so 57% of the room was one flat colour.* **Fixed by deriving rather than inventing**: *fixtures from the game's own metatile behaviours (128 counter, 130 shelf, 157 window…), wall kinds from what vanilla drew there (**27 hotel wall ids collapse to five kinds** by colour signature), and the two rooms given separate grounds because vanilla does — commonest colour 56.8% → 23.5%.* ***And a chair landed on an NPC***: *drawing chairs on open floor put one under a T-119 local at (1,2); `_map_events()` now derives every warp/object/sign cell from each map.json's own `layout` field, since the layout id does not name its map and mangling only works by luck.* ***The second batch taught three more, and all three were the same mistake — a derived rule applied too bluntly***: *(1) **a window is an OPENING, not a colour** — the classifier keys on blue, so the Game Corner's sky band and the whole store roof came out a field of panes; when windows are more than a third of a room's wall they are not windows, and they are demoted to whatever that room's wall is otherwise made of (it fired on exactly three rooms — roof 47.8%, Game Corner 47.9%, prize room 43.5% — and left the condominiums' real windows at 12–18% alone); (2) **"dark" is the surround vanilla leaves OUTSIDE a room, never a wall material** — letting it win the demotion vote turned that sky band into black slabs; (3) **each trade needs its own GROUND** — a third warm neutral made the condominiums look unfinished, and cool stone against the store's parquet and the hotel's carpet is what made them read as three buildings.* **And two grounds needed their own pattern rather than `_weave`: the Game Corner is a CHEQUER (a grid you stand inside) and the landings are TILED (grout on an 8px grid).** *One misdiagnosis corrected on the way: the black bars down the condominiums' landings are vanilla's own, not a bug of ours.* ***THE SCHOOL IS SPLIT PER TOWN, decided 2026-09-16, and that closes this ticket***: **one tileset served two rooms in two towns doing two different things** — *`VIRIDIAN_CITY_SCHOOL` is **CALLOW's** classroom and `CELADON_CITY_CONDOMINIUMS_ROOF_ROOM` is **VERDIGRIS's** lecture room, which is not a classroom at all: it reads you a DAEMON Lecture and hands over a MUSAI.* **Their plans are all but identical** (*windows at (1,1), a region map at (7,1), bookshelves at (8,2), a long table at (4,4), the mat at (4,7); the lecture room adds a lectern at (7,3)*), **so the split is about TOWN and FUNCTION, not layout** — *a tileset apiece, as `checkpoint_indigo` already serves exactly one layout.* ***CALLOW's classroom is built of its own BENCHMARK's materials***: **poured concrete under a surveyor's grid (T-113), so THE GRID ON THE BOARD IS THE GRID UNDER YOUR FEET** — *the type table drawn as a lattice of identical chalk cells, the desks facing it, and the one living thing in the room a plant on the sill grown past its cane, which nobody remarks on.* **The room's own shipped text was already the lesson**: *"I have to have the table by Friday. I do not have to understand it. I have to have it." / "It is all on the board." / "Learn the table. It does not change."* ***VERDIGRIS's lecture room is built of the block it sits on top of*** — *cool stone landings, bronze fittings, verdigris only where a hand goes — and its board is a lecture board rather than a table to be learned.* **`gTileset_CallowSchool` 16 blocks / 43 tiles, `gTileset_VerdigrisLecture` 13 / 26, 0 collision changes in either, and `gTileset_School` now has ZERO consumers.** ***THE SCHOOL SPANS TWO TOWNS AND THAT IS OPEN***: *`VIRIDIAN_CITY_SCHOOL` is **CALLOW's** and `CELADON_CITY_CONDOMINIUMS_ROOF_ROOM` is Verdigris's — and the second is not a schoolroom but a **lecture room** (it hands out a MUSAI). luminance lines the ramp up (l 178 / J 111 / N 54 against LEAF 169 / DARK 110 / DEEP 71).* ***The rocks go grey, and that is a judgment call with nothing in the bible ruling on it***: **`item_ball.png` — ours, 16×16, in the same `npc_white` slot — spends four colours and no dither, a hard `K` rim over flat `w`/`g`/`G` bands**, *which is also SLATE's vocabulary; vanilla's olive-and-sand dither reads as mossy earth.* **The rock is angular and fissured and the boulder smooth and round, so the two never read as one object** — *one is broken open, the other shoved aside.* **Reversible: revert this file's ROCK and BOULDER art and rerun.** ***THE FIRST "REDRAW" WAS A TRANSCRIPTION AND WAS DISCARDED***: **it came out 248 of 256 cells identical to vanilla — twelve of sixteen rows byte for byte** — *which is not a conversion, and would have put Nintendo-derived pixels into THIS repo as source literals, the one thing `CLAUDE.md` promises not to do.* **`gbatrees.py` and `gbaground.py` do derive from vanilla pixel by pixel, but they READ it out of `engineGba` at runtime; ROCKER skunk for JUGGLER octopus ×3; WOMAN_2 ewe for POKEMON_BREEDER goose ×2; HIKER badger for RUIN_MANIAC aardvark ×2; and COOLTRAINER_M wolf ×1 and COOLTRAINER_F falcon ×1 both stand for COOL_COUPLE's two ibexes.** *A first pass reported the 47 CORPUS STAFF trainers as mismatched too and that was a FALSE ALARM — their portraits are drawn under the job names `corpus_m`/`corpus_f` rather than `p_rocket_grunt_*`, and both sides are the same penguins; a parser that keys on a naming convention finds a mismatch wherever the convention was not followed.* ***Decided 2026-09-16***: **the four single classes get overworld sheets of their own** (*weasel, octopus, goose, aardvark — all above T-120's ≥3-trainer cut-off at 7, 6 and 8 trainers, so they have earned drawings*), **and COOL_COUPLE, which is a PAIR class standing as two separate single sprites, has its portrait redrawn as the two animals it actually stands as — a wolf and a falcon** — **shipped: `burglar` (weasel), `juggler` (octopus), `pokemon_breeder` (goose) and `ruin_maniac` (aardvark), four NEW nine-frame sheets registered as their own `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_*`, and the ten objects that BATTLE as one of them repointed across seven maps** (*Poké Mansion 2F/3F/B1F, Silph 5F, Victory Road 2F, Pattern Bush, Lost Cave*). ***Each sheet inherits the SLOT of the sprite it replaces*** — *pink/2 from the maniac, blue/1 from the rocker, white/4 from the woman and the hiker* — **because the engine patches the palette named by a sprite's tag INTO the slot named by its info, so two sprites sharing a slot with different tags repaint each other**; *the palette was therefore already loaded on every map they stand on, each figure is drawn from the sixteen that slot already has, and no colour is added anywhere* (**engine.md trap 17**). **The couple's portrait is redrawn instead of given a sheet** — *a pair sheet would serve one battle in the game and leave the wolf and the falcon still wrong* — **and nothing below the collar changed: rows 0–23 hold head and nothing else, so the two heads are cleared and redrawn on the couple's own palette.** ***The falcon took four drafts and the fix was to TURN THE HEAD***: *a beak pointed at the viewer is a nose, the cheeks either side of it are a face, and every front-facing draft came out a person with a bowl cut* — **in profile the hooked wedge is a bird and nothing else.** *Verified by DECODE in all four ROMs, not by byte-match: a scrambled sheet matches its own scrambled self.* **Left open for T-120: the cooltrainer portraits themselves are still vanilla's purple-haired humans, and the 38-pairing join is the check that will close it** ***CLOSED TOO EARLY, AND THE REASON IS THE TICKET'S OWN LESSON WEARING DIFFERENT CLOTHES***: **the join globbed `data/maps/*/scripts.inc`, which holds 258 of the game's 726 battle labels** — *`data/scripts/trainers.inc` is ONE shared file holding 468 more, which is every route and island trainer there is.* **It did not fail. It reported 38 pairings where there are 72, five disagreements where there are sixteen, and the repointing that followed was a third of the job** (*engine.md **trap 18***). ***Widened and finished 2026-09-16***: **eleven more classes drawn and registered — `BIRD_KEEPER` an ostrich (18 objects, the largest disagreement in the game), `GAMER` a jackal (6), `SUPER_NERD` a lemur, `PAINTER` a toucan, `AROMA_LADY` a honeybee (4 each), `PSYCHIC_F` a jellyfish, `POKEMON_RANGER_M` and `_F` one pine marten in two uniforms (3 each), `LADY` a swan, `ENGINEER` a beaver, `TAMER` a hyena (2 each)** — *every one of their pictures already existed; what was missing was the sprite.* **60 more objects repointed, 16 sheets in all.** ***The honeybee came out a BULL first*** — *`mammal()` puts an ear at each top corner and two straight stalks over a tan face are horns, which engine.md had already recorded twice* — **so it is drawn without a mammal head: compound eyes taking the sides of the whole face, antennae two rows tall ending in a KNOB, which is what a horn never does.** ***And the portrait census was wrong in the other direction too***: *of the 79 still on vanilla art, **exactly ONE is reachable** — `PSYCHIC_M`, TRAINER_PSYCHIC_DARIO in the SEVEN ISLAND TRAINER TOWER, with a VS Seeker rematch; the other 78 are named in `trainers.h` and nowhere else.* **He is repainted from our own `psychic_f` rather than drafted** (*no tool here can post to `daemon/sprite`; this is `genbacks.py`'s method — keep the six colours that are the jellyfish, re-dye the nine that are cloth*), **and the first dye came out a bright rose because it lifted red; value is what the muted register is made of, so the second takes the ramp DOWN into an indigo.** ***`tools/spritejoin.py` is the check that should have existed first***: it prints **what it examined before what it found**, and its test is *names OR species* — *matching names alone reported 47 CORPUS STAFF trainers as mismatched, matching species alone reported every town's staff against itself.* **Disagreements: 16 pairings over 68 objects → ONE over 8. | ~~**T-182**~~ | ~~**HELP could not be closed, and would have blacked out the overworld**~~ (*found driving the build in the theatre, 2026-09-21*) — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (engine; `start_menu.c`; T-179): **the START menu polls a chosen entry's callback EVERY FRAME until it returns TRUE**, *and T-179's HELP entry sets its request and returns FALSE* — ***so the request was set again on the frame the Help System closed, which re-opened it, forever.*** **B could not get out of HELP and neither could START; only a reset could**, and *this shipped in all four ROMs.* **Fixed by asking once and handing the menu back to `StartCB_HandleInput`** — *`main()` skips the game's callbacks while help is up, so the menu simply waits underneath, which is what the entry was written to do.* ***A second fault underneath it***: **HELP was absent from `StartMenu_FadeScreenIfLeavingOverworld`'s exception list**, *so picking it faded the overworld to black behind a screen that draws its own* — **exactly the bug the comment beside that list already describes for DEBUG**, which is the second time this list has cost a screen. ***Neither would have been found by building***: *both compile, and only pressing B shows either.* | `engineGba/src/start_menu.c` | T-179 |
| ~~**T-184**~~ | ~~**The daemons' outlines are eighteen different blacks**~~ (*the user's, 2026-09-21, after T-177's true black reached the portraits and not the creatures*) — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (`tools/gbasprite.py`; 9.4 amended, v11.229): **measured off the screen rather than the file** — *a GROWTH daemon's outline reads `(24, 33, 24)`, a dark green.* ***9.4 kept a trace of the hue at level 0*** on the reasoning that **an outline that takes the hue stops reading as an outline**, *and the answer taken from that was "a trace is not the hue".* **It is** — *and across eighteen types it made eighteen blacks, none of which reads as black beside a portrait carrying the real one.* ***The lesson survives the reversal***: the guarantee is to give the outline no hue at all. **`ramp5` ends on `INK = (16, 16, 20)`, the same constant `gbaoutline.py` uses**, *so a daemon and the person throwing it are edged in one colour.* **454 sprites rewritten; the hue is carried by the three entries whose job it is.** | `tools/gbasprite.py` | T-177, 9.4, invariant 5 |
| ~~**T-185**~~ | ~~**The Index shows you a whole evolution line you have never met**~~ (*the user's, 2026-09-21: "only the daemons you have (own or seen) should be illuminated"*) — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (`pokedex_screen.c`; 4.2, v11.229): **`DexScreen_DrawMonPicInCategoryPage` draws the picture AND the name with no seen gate at all** — *only the caught ball is gated* — **and a category page groups an evolution line**, so *meeting the first of a chain showed the rest of it for free.* ***Checked against upstream before touching it: identical, so this is vanilla's own behaviour and not a regression of ours.*** **Vanilla already knows the rule and breaks it here**: *the list two screens away prints `gText_5Dashes` for an unseen daemon, and pressing A on an unseen one on this page already does nothing.* ***Ours repaints the loaded pic's palette to one ink*** — **`RGB(2, 2, 2)`, the same black T-184 just gave the sprites** — *so an unseen daemon is exactly its own outline filled in*, **names it `-----` in the game's own idiom, and keeps the number**, *because where a thing sits in the Index is not what binding one earns you.* | `engineGba/src/pokedex_screen.c` | T-184, 4.2 |
| ~~**T-184a**~~ | ~~**A black line across four figures**~~ (*the user, playing the build 2026-09-21: "crystals sprite has a black line in her middle"*) — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (`tools/gbaoutline.py`): **`gbaoutline` splits every picture into 64-row frames because a BACK PIC is a strip of stacked 64×64 frames**, *and outlining a strip whole inks the seams where one frame's feet meet the next one's head.* ***The four intro pictures are 64×96*** — **one figure, not a frame and a half** — *so the split drew an outline straight across them at the cut.* **Rows 63 and 64 carried three times the dark pixels of any other row, in all four**: CRYSTAL, the player in both poses, and the rival. ***A strip's height is a MULTIPLE of 64***; anything else is one picture and is outlined whole. *The four restored from before the pass and redrawn — edge in the darkest four goes **43% → 100%**, seam rows ordinary again.* **`gbachar.py`'s own outline pass was checked and is clean**: it works on the cut cell, so it never had the seam. | `tools/gbaoutline.py` | T-177, T-184 |
| ~~**T-185a**~~ | ~~**T-185's premise was wrong, and the gap was the point**~~ (*the user's field test, 2026-09-21: "if I have only seen the base evolution I do not see the silhouettes for other unseen evolutions and I thought I should"*) — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (`pokedex_screen.c`): ***I read the draw site and not the fill site.*** **`DexScreen_DrawMonPicInCategoryPage` has no seen-gate on the picture or the name, which is why T-185 called it a leak** — *but `DexScreen_GetPageMons` only puts a species on the page **if** `FLAG_GET_SEEN`, so the draw function is never handed an unseen one and T-185's silhouette was **dead code**.* ***What vanilla actually does is worse than a leak***: **an unseen daemon is dropped from the page and the survivors close up**, *so a page of four you have half-met looks like a page of two and says nothing about the rest.* **A category page holds a habitat group, which is usually one or two whole evolution lines** — *`Grassland` page 1 is RATTATA·RATICATE·SENTRET·FURRET, page 3 the whole NIDORAN♀ line* — ***so the gap IS the information: it says the line goes on.*** **The unseen are kept on the page now and the silhouette finally runs**: *a black shape, named `-----`, number intact, and opening one still refused as it always was.* ***Also answers the user's DEADLETTER / EXHAUST confusion***: **a page is a HABITAT group, not one chain** — *88 and 109 are two separate lines sharing a page, the way RATTATA and SENTRET do.* | `engineGba/src/pokedex_screen.c` | T-185, 4.2 |
| ~~**T-186**~~ | **PING is missing a beak, and may be the wrong daemon for that moment** (*the user, playing 2026-09-21*) **Two things in one note.** *The drawing first* — **the bird has no beak**, *which at 64px is the one feature that makes a bird a bird* (**T-176's standing negative about dropped features, one size up**). *And second, a question the ticket should not answer for itself*: **is PING still the right daemon to meet at that point in the game?** *It was chosen before the bestiary was finished.* ***Closes when*** *the drawing has a beak and the user has ruled on whether PING stays in that slot.* | `gfx/daemons/`, `tools/gbasprite.py` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21***: ***it had no beak at all*** — *not a thin one, none* — **and at 64px the one feature that makes a bird a bird was simply absent**, *which is why it could be seen to be wrong without being sayable.* ***A ping is a needle you poke at something and wait on***, **so the beak is the daemon as much as it is the bird.** *Drawn into the source and re-ramped; only spearow's five files moved.* ***The second half of the ticket stands***: **whether PING is still the right daemon to meet at that point is the user's ruling, not a tool's.** | T-176, 9.4 |
| ~~**T-187**~~ | **THRASHING shows nothing on the healthbox** (*the user, playing 2026-09-21: "I didnt see any status indicator"*) ***Not a bug — vanilla's healthbox only ever shows the five MAJOR statuses*** (`SLP` `PSN` `BRN` `PRZ` `FRZ`), *and confusion is volatile, announced in the log and never drawn.* **But this game has a stronger reason to draw it than vanilla does**: *our statuses are **states of mind**, THRASHING is 1.6's rename of confusion, and a player who cannot see that their daemon is in one cannot reason about it.* ***The cost is a sprite slot and a healthbox redraw***, and there is a design question under it: **if THRASHING gets a mark, do the other volatile states** (*LATCH, ENCLOSE, the counters*) **get one too, and does the healthbox then become a dashboard?** ***Closes when*** *a rule is written for which states are visible and why, and the visible ones are drawn.* | `engineGba/src/battle_interface.c` ***SCOPED 2026-09-21, NOT STARTED***: **this is an ART ticket wearing engine clothes.** *`UpdateStatusIconInHealthbox` chains on `STATUS1_*` and confusion is `STATUS2_CONFUSION`, a VOLATILE flag the healthbox is never asked about* — **so it needs a new 32×8 icon in `graphics/battle_interface`, a slot in the `B_INTERFACE_GFX_*` table, a palette, AND a new trigger**, *because a volatile status changing does not call the updater at all.* ***And the design question is the real one***: **a mark for THRASHING invites marks for LATCH, ENCLOSE and the counters, at which point the healthbox is a dashboard** — *which is a different game's screen.* ***Closes when*** *a rule is written for which states are visible and why, and the visible ones are drawn.* — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-22*** (v11.233): ***the ticket was waiting on a design question, not on code*** — **does marking a volatile state turn the healthbox into a dashboard?** *The answer is a line that means something:* ***the healthbox NAMES what persists and MARKS what does not.*** **The five that follow a daemon out of a battle stay words; THRASHING, which ends when the fight does, is a sawtooth with no letters** — *the difference in KIND drawn rather than stated, and the tag space kept for things that keep.* ***It also settles an accident***: **THRASHING's three letters are taken — `THR` is THROTTLED.** *It went in the three tiles `battle_interface.c` has said were unused since vanilla, so **nothing else in the sheet moves**; it borrows SUS's pill so only the inside differs; and `gbagfx` converts this sheet by `index % 16`, which every existing icon confirms.* ***BATTLER0 only, and that is a real limit***: **each battler draws a differently shaped badge and 36-38 is the only free slot** — *the other three need a fourth row of tiles, which changes an INCBIN'd blob's size and wants a screen first.* **And something had to ASK**: *nothing calls the updater when a volatile status changes, so the action menu does, once a turn.* ***T-187a, 2026-09-22 — the limit is gone, and it was a misreading.*** **The four per-battler status icons are NOT four shapes — they are one shape in four PALETTE SLOTS.** *`UpdateStatusIconInHealthbox` does `pltAdder += battlerId + 12` and fills a single entry, so battler 0's ground is nibble 12 and battler 3's is 15.* **A pixel comparison said "battler 0's icons are not battler 1's" and I read that as different art**; *the tool found the truth by refusing to map nibble 13.* ***The sheet gains a fourth row*** (`320x24 → 320x32`) **and all four battlers show the mark** — *safe because `gBattleInterface_Gfx` is declared `[][32]` and indexed by tile, and nothing anywhere counts them.* | 1.6, 4.2 |
| **T-210** | **113 Index entries are still vanilla's prose, and that is what blocks the last margins** (*found writing them, 2026-09-22*) ***OPUS covers 240 daemons: every one whose entry is OURS.*** **The remaining 113 cannot have a margin, and the reason is not effort** — *a margin answers our entry, and annotating someone else's writing is a note in the margin of a page nobody here wrote.* ***They are also all UNREACHABLE***, *which is why T-40 left them: it wrote the thirty that a player could meet and stopped, on the same reachability standard T-120 and T-131 use.* **So this ticket is a record rather than a request**: *the margins are complete by the project's own rule, and the only way to 240 → 386 runs through 113 Index entries in the authored voice* — ***which is an entry ticket wearing a margin ticket's clothes, and a decision rather than a job.*** ***And a trap it re-found***: **comparing our entries to upstream byte-for-byte counts SWEPT vanilla as ours** — *61 by that measure, 33 once the vocabulary is applied to vanilla first, which is exactly what T-40 wrote down and I did anyway.* | `src/data/pokemon/pokedex_text_*.h` ***CORRECTED 2026-09-22, and the correction matters.*** **The blocker is not the entries — it is the NAMES.** *Of the 171 daemons without a margin, **170 still carry vanilla's own name*** (`ABSOL`, `AGGRON`, `ALTARIA`, `ARON`, `BAGON`…), **so writing them an Index entry would mean writing about a creature this project has not named yet.** ***That is T-45 and T-171's work and it cascades***: **2.10's rule is that naming a daemon brings its learnset into scope**, *which is how T-45's thirty became fifty-two and T-46's thirty-two became twenty-three.* ***So the chain is naming → entry → margin***, **and the margin is the last link and the cheapest one.** *This ticket is a record of where the bestiary stops, not a job waiting to be picked up.* | T-40, T-120, T-197 |

| ~~**T-190**~~ | **The perspective should say PERSPECTIVE, and should do more once you are there** (*the user's field test, 2026-09-21*) **Four things, and they are one system.** *(1) The hint reads* ***"R looks across"***, *and should use the word the thesis uses* — **`L reads.` / `R: PERSPECTIVE.` measures 45px and 88px in a 112px prompt, so it fits.** *(2)* **A cursor arrow sits on your own daemon in the perspective view and A does nothing** — *either it acts or it goes.* *(3)* ***The elegant fix the user found***: **L already reads the daemon opposite you — so once R has moved you across, L should read YOUR daemon**, *same button, same meaning, the viewpoint having changed underneath it.* *(4)* **SELECT shows STATS with their modifiers** (*attack, speed, and a ±n where a routine has moved one*), **obeying the same rule**: *whoever is across from where you are standing.* ***The rule to write down***: **your viewpoint decides who "they" is; L reads them and SELECT measures them.** *That is teachable in one fight and it is the argument in the controls.* ***Closes when*** *all four are in and the rule is in 4.2.* | `engineGba/src/battle_controller_player.c` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (`battle_controller_player.c`; 4.2): ***one rule, and the buttons obey it — your viewpoint decides who "they" is.*** **L already opened the Index for the daemon opposite; R moves you to the other side, so from over there the daemon opposite is YOUR OWN and L reads that instead.** *Same button, same meaning, the viewpoint moved underneath it* — **the user's own idea, and better than the one it replaced** (*A on a cursor*) *because it needs no new control and states the argument in the controls rather than beside them.* **SELECT measures whoever is opposite**: *Gen 3 keeps stats UNMODIFIED and applies stages at damage time, so the stored number and its stage are exactly "what it started with and what has happened to it since".* ***Four lines across the two windows that exist during action selection*** — **the pairs go in the 112px prompt, because the worst case `ATK 255+6  DEF 255-6` is 102px and would not fit the 96px menu beside it.** ***And the arrow that did nothing is gone***: *the action menu's cursor is a SPRITE and survived into the look, sitting on a window that now holds prose.* **The debug theatre's door moved from SELECT to START.** | T-180, T-180a, 4.2 |
| ~~**T-191**~~ | **Colour is the type and nothing ever names it** (*the user, 2026-09-21: "this would allow the player to learn what colors link to what type"*) ***9.4 claims hue is a label a player can READ***, **and the game asserts it everywhere and states it almost nowhere** — *a player sees PACKET is blue and CODEMUSAI is red and has no way to check what that means at the moment they are looking.* **The cheapest and most on-theme place is the L = READ page**: *reading is where you learn, the page already has the daemon in front of you, and the Index entry deliberately has no type field* (***4.2: the artifact can only measure content***) — **so this is a real design question and not a layout one: does the type belong on the entry, or beside it?** *The battle screen itself is the other candidate.* ***Closes when*** *the type is legible somewhere a player can reach mid-fight, without breaking 4.2's claim that the Index cannot hold what matters.* | `engineGba/src/pokedex_screen.c`, `battle_controller_player.c` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (`pokedex_screen.c`; 4.2, 9.4, v11.230): ***the entry page stated the type twice and named it never*** — **once in the daemon's own colouring and once in T-175's mark** — *so the label 9.4 claims a player can read was legible only to someone already told.* **The word sits beside the mark at FONT_SMALL** (*the HT line is 42px, the mark begins at 88, and the widest of the eighteen names is 40*) **and is gated exactly as the mark is** — `DexScreen_DrawMonFootprint` *refuses an uncaught daemon* — ***so the mark and its name arrive together and each teaches the other.*** **It names the PRIMARY type**, *which is what the mark and the colour encode; a second named here would label something the page never shows.* ***And the battle hint now names both screens***: **`L INDEX.` / `R PERSPECTIVE.`**, *47px and 83px in a 112px prompt* (**the first piece of T-190**). | 9.4, 4.2, T-190 |
| ~~**T-192**~~ | **The START menu has one row too many since HELP joined it** (*the user, 2026-09-21*) **T-179 added HELP as an entry and the menu now runs past what the screen holds comfortably.** ***The user's suggestion***: *drop the full-width description banner and keep only the menu.* **Worth measuring before deciding** — *the banner is where every entry explains itself, and the debug build adds a row the release build does not, so the release menu may not overflow at all.* ***Closes when*** *the release menu is measured and either fits or is reorganised.* | `engineGba/src/start_menu.c` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21***: **eight rows do not fit and the arithmetic is not close.** *`CreateStartMenuWindow` makes a window of `(items * 2 - 1)` tiles at `tilemapTop` 1 and `DrawStdWindowFrame` borders it either side, so **eight items occupy rows 0 to 16 while the description banner starts at 15**.* **Seven is vanilla's number and vanilla's fit exactly.** ***`EXIT` is the row that costs nothing***: *B and START both close the menu, and every other row does something a player cannot do another way.* ***The alternative is recorded in the code and is the user's to take***: **keep EXIT and cut all six vanilla descriptions to one line** — *they run 370–406px against a 224px line, so that is six rewrites of translated copy rather than a layout fix.* | T-179 |
| ~~**T-193**~~ | **BAG is the last plain-English container in the register** (*the user, 2026-09-21*) *Everything else a player carries has been renamed into the lexicon and the bag has not.* ***Recommended: `HEAP`*** — **it is exactly "the place a running process keeps what it allocated"**, *it is the STACK's counterpart so it carries a second true meaning*, **and at 18px it is the same width as BAG**, *which matters because the battle menu's first column is 56px and the word appears in a dozen fixed panes.* *Considered and weaker: STORE (retail), CONTAINER (too long), BUFFER (transient — wrong, a bag is not), POOL, REGISTRY.* ***Closes when*** *the name is chosen, checked for collisions with `check_lexicon`, and swept.* | `tools/port_vocab.py`, `src/data/text/` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21***: ***`POOL`, not `HEAP`.*** **HEAP is wrong twice over** — *it is already what GEODUDE is called, and its own Index entry is the reason not to use it*: **"Things are put down on it and left. It holds all of them. It has never been asked to give one back."** ***A bag is the one container that DOES give things back***, *and `STACK` sits right behind HEAP spending the other half of that joke.* **A POOL is exactly what a bag is: a set of reusable things you draw from and return to.** *Swept through `port_vocab` rather than by hand because POOL is 24px against BAG's 18 — 23 files, 41 dialogue blocks, 17 src literals, 2 json, no line over its pane, and one fixed-width array caught by the tool.* | 2.8, 1.6 |
| ~~**T-194**~~ | **CACHE is both the money and a daemon** (*the user, 2026-09-21*) **Two things a player reads constantly share one word.** ***The daemon should keep it***: *a daemon's name is earned by its Index entry and cannot be swapped without the entry moving too, where the money is a UI word with no argument attached.* ***Recommended for the money: `BUDGET`*** — **the game is about optimising for a flattened metric, and a budget is the flattened metric people actually live inside**, *so the word does work rather than just avoiding a collision.* *Considered: CREDIT (keeps the compute pun, says less), QUOTA (good, colder), FUNDS (flat), ALLOCATION (too long for the pane).* **`CACHE` was chosen as a pun on cash and the pun is the weakest thing about it.** ***Closes when*** *the money is renamed, the pane widths are checked, and `check_lexicon` is clean.* | `tools/port_vocab.py`, `src/data/text/` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21***: **the daemon keeps CACHE and the money becomes `BUDGET`.** *A daemon's name is earned by its Index entry; this was a label with no argument attached.* ***BUDGET has one***: **the game is about optimising for a flattened metric, and a budget is the flattened metric people actually live inside.** *Changed by hand and deliberately NOT put in `VOCAB` — an entry for CACHE would rename the daemon the moment any dialogue mentions it.* **What is lost is recorded**: *CACHE carried a hoard, a fast local memory and cash at once, which was one more reading than the lexicon asks of any term.* | 1.6, 2.8 |

Vanilla never makes 386 at once, so it never paid for it.* **Now each is made at level 5 and given level 50's experience** — *a box keeps experience, not a level* — **509 frames, about 8½ seconds.** *Played: ROVERCUB in box 1 reads Lv50, "Met in BLANCHE TOWN at Lv 40", so OPUS's margins still read both ways; the party six are unchanged.* ***Then cut again, the same night (engine `aaf5122a3`)***: **8½ seconds of black is still a hang on a phone, and the cost — boxed daemons with level-5 routines — was not one worth keeping.** **`DaemonsDebug_CreateBoxMonFast` writes each box record directly** *(species, EXP from the growth table, the newest four level-up moves at or below 50, met data, IVs, ability)* **and checksums and encrypts it once: the black after the last A is now about 105 frames, under two seconds.** *Played: box 1 is 30 valid Lv50 daemons, no Bad Eggs; ROVERCUB met at Lv 40, ROVERSEER at Lv 50 with SPLIT, ADVERTISE, SCALE UP and REGROW and 117,360 EXP.* **Not yet re-tested on Delta — the user's to confirm.** | T-205, T-228 |

| ~~**T-200**~~ | **The type should be its COLOUR on the Index entry, not just its name** (*the user's field test, 2026-09-21: "so we can link the daemon's color with the type color"*) **T-191 put the type's NAME on the entry and that is half the job** — *9.4 claims hue is a label a player can read, and a player still cannot see the hue and the word in one place.* ***The game already draws coloured type chips***: **`BlitMenuInfoIcon(windowId, type + 1, x, y)`**, *which `pokemon_summary_screen.c` uses on its INFO and MOVES pages.* ***What stops it being a five-line change***: **the chip needs its own palette loaded at the target window's offset**, *and every window on the entry page is spoken for* — **the stats and entry windows are palette 0 (the page's text) and the picture window is palette 9 (the daemon's own colours)** — *so it wants a NEW window with a free palette slot and a `baseBlock` past `0x322`, placed where nothing else draws.* ***And that placement cannot be settled off the screen***, which is what this ticket is waiting for. ***Closes when*** *a player can see the hue, the mark and the word together.* | `engineGba/src/pokedex_screen.c` | `src/pokedex_screen.c`, `src/data/type_colours.h` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-22***: **both types, each in its own colour**, *from `sTypeTextColor` — the same table the move menu prints a move's name with, moved into a header of its own so a type cannot be one colour in battle and another in the Index.* ***The palette was the whole problem and palette 1 was the answer***: **all sixteen of palette 0's slots belong to the dex chrome**, *but palette 1 is black in `kanto_dex_bgpals` and nothing on the page draws with it* — **the entry window copies palette 0 into it and paints two slots**, *so every existing colour renders exactly as before and the two new ones cost the chrome nothing.* ***And it now names BOTH types***, **which vanilla did and we had dropped** — *a dual type is the case where the chart is hardest to hold in your head, so showing one of them threw away the half that needed showing.* |

what is missing is a button and somewhere to put its label.* **The entry page's control row is spoken for** — *START plays the cry, SELECT turns the page over (T-197), A is next and B cancels* — ***so this needs a button decision rather than code.*** ***Closes when*** *an entry can get to its own page.* | ***CLOSED 2026-09-24*** *(built 2026-09-23, kept by the user's pick; | ~~**T-203**~~ | **Nothing says OPUS is why the margin is there** (*the user's, 2026-09-21: "otherwise how would anyone know?"*) **A player who finds `SELECT MARGIN` on an entry learns that the button exists and never learns what wrote the line** — *and the object that did it is sitting in their POOL with a description that says so.* ***The restraint is deliberate and the ticket is not asking to break it***: *nobody remarks on OPUS and who left it is never answered.* **But there is a difference between not explaining it and giving the player nothing to connect** — *and the cheapest honest connection is the one beat that was already planned and is not built*: ***"Something has been written in the INDEX."***, **the first time a margin appears.** *That is a player looking at their POOL two minutes later and working it out.* ***It needs the only new save state in the whole idea***: **a bit per species for "already announced"**, *or one bit for "the first one has happened", which is cheaper and probably enough.* ***Closes when*** *a player can find out, without being told.* | `engineGba/src/pokedex_screen.c` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-22*** (v11.233): ***"Something has been written in the INDEX."*** — **once, ever, on the first step after a margin exists to read, and it does not name itself.** *The same step hook the egg and the STREAM use, for the same reason: the first step after the world changed is when the player is listening.* ***PARTY ONLY, on purpose***: **this runs on every step and the boxes are 420 mons where the party is six.** *A first margin earned by a BOXED daemon goes unannounced, which is the right way round for a cheap check to be wrong — **the first one a player earns is one they were carrying**.* | T-197 |
| ~~**T-204**~~ | ~~**Three HEARSAY names are clipped**~~ (*the user's field test, 2026-09-21*) — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21***: **the list window is eight tiles and held vanilla's names, not ours.** *Text prints at x=8 behind the cursor, so that is **56px** — and of the sixteen hearsay names **CRYSTAL CLEAR is 78px, MELANCHOLIC 66 and PHLEGMATIC 60**.* ***Ten tiles is the most it can take, and the limit is the icons***: **they are placed at `47 * (i % 3) + 0x72`, so the first column's centre is 114 and a 32px sprite starts at 98**, *where a ten-tile window's frame ends at 96.* **Eleven would put the frame under the icons, and sprites draw over windows.** *80px of room against a 78px widest name — recorded, because that is two pixels.* | `engineGba/src/fame_checker.c` | T-119 |

| ~~**T-211**~~ | **The TOOLKIT was teaching vanilla's colour chart** (*found 2026-09-22, checking what a PLUGIN looks like after T-208*) **All 58 PLUGINs and DRIVERs share ONE drawing (`gItemIcon_TMHM`) and are told apart only by a per-type palette**, *which makes the TOOLKIT the one screen in the game that shows eighteen hues beside eighteen type names at once* — ***a colour chart whether or not anyone designed it as one.*** **And it was vanilla's**: *VECTOR is our red and its disc was pale blue; LOGIC is steel blue and its disc was orange; OPAQUE is near-black and its disc was pale cyan; SIGNAL is cyan and its disc was yellow.* ***A player building exactly the intuition 9.4 claims they can build would have built it backwards***, **from our own screen, using our own names** — *which is worse than teaching nothing.* | `tools/genplugincolours.py`, `engineGba/graphics/items/icon_palettes/` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-22***: **sixteen palettes re-ramped from `gbasprite.py`'s `TYPE_COLOR`**, *read at run time rather than copied, so a disc and the creature it teaches about cannot disagree.* **Vanilla's structure is kept** — *entries 3–9 are the type's own seven-step ramp, lightest first; the rest is the disc's shading.* ***The second half nobody had checked***: **each disc carries the type of the routine ACTUALLY on it** (`sTMHMMoves` → `battle_moves.h` → `item_icon_table.h`) — *all 58 did, and it is checked now, because a retyped routine would teach a wrong pair and nothing else in the build would notice.* **`check_lexicon.check_plugin_colours()` guards both halves**, *proved by breaking both at once.* | T-208, 9.4 |
