| ~~**T-183**~~ | **`the foe` is vanilla's word for a thing** (*the user's, 2026-09-21, out of T-181: "'them' could be used for most of them if it buys a bit more and allows a better writing"*). ***It does both.*** **`the foe` is 40px and `them` is 23px**, and the saving is not the argument — *this game spends forty hours insisting the thing across from you has an inside, and `them` asserts that in every routine description a player reads without ever saying the thesis (craft rule 1).* **135 occurrences in `move_descriptions.c` and ten in everything else**, *so finishing the job costs almost nothing once it is started.* ***The catch is the prize***: **most are vanilla's PASSIVE** — *"The foe is hit with an intense flame"* — **and a blind swap writes "Them is hit with"**, so each has to be turned ACTIVE: *"Hits them with an intense flame"*, **132px → 93px, and better English.** ***So it is ~135 sentences of writing and not a regex***, which is why it is a ticket. **T-181's fourteen are done this way already** and three of them came back to T-138's own wording with only the pronoun changed. ***Closes when*** *no player-facing routine, ability or battle line calls a daemon `the foe`, a tool FLAGS each passive rather than rewriting it, and `port_vocab` still reports zero.* `engineGba/src/move_descriptions.c`, `tools/` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-22***: **154 routine descriptions, six ability descriptions and the help system.** *Zero `foe` left in anything a player can read, zero lines over the 113px pane.* ***The 37 passives were turned ACTIVE by hand***, *which is why it is a table and not a regular expression* — **and why they read better afterwards: an active sentence is shorter, and these have four lines to live in.** ***THEN THE OUTPUT HAD TO BE READ, TWICE***, **which is the finding**: *a rule that turns "the foe" into "them" writes* **"takes what them was holding"**, *and a rule that leaves "it" alone writes* **"holds it there"** *about a someone it has just called them.* **Singular they needs the verb to agree AND the other pronouns in the sentence to follow it, and neither is something a substitution can know.** *19 found on the first read, 7 more on the second —* ***"Copies the routine them last ran" survived the first scan because "last" is not a verb a pattern thinks to look for*** — *and six say `foe` with no article at all ("all foe", "Prevents foe from"), which is vanilla's clipped plural and matches no rule.* | T-181, T-138, 1.6 ***T-183a, 2026-09-22 — A FOURTH SURFACE.*** **The TOOLKIT prints the ITEM's description and not the routine's** (`ItemId_GetDescription`, `tm_case.c`), *so all fifty PLUGINs and eight DRIVERs described themselves in vanilla's prose on a screen whose every other word is ours* — **and 32 of the last `foe`s were living there.** ***The fix is not fifty more descriptions***: **a PLUGIN is the routine, so the item takes the routine's own words** (`tools/port_plugin_text.py`) *and cannot drift from them again.* **With the three held items, the ten in each edition's Index entries and one line of the tutorial: zero `foe` anywhere a player can read.** | T-181, T-138, 1.6 |
| ~~**T-181**~~ | **Two tools disagree about how wide the description pane is** (*found sweeping tool drift, 2026-09-21*). **`port_vocab` reports 22 move descriptions as "more lines than the pane shows" and would rewrap every one of them** — *T-138 wrote those lines and measured them; the tool would also add a SCROLL BREAK to two files that never had one, which its own report says out loud.* ***The same disagreement showed up in the journals***: *`port_vocab` wrapped "Special Feature: DOLDRUM / BENCHMARK LEADER BASIN!" and `port_hearsay` wrapped it back the other way, every run.* **One of the two width models is wrong and the other is right, and nothing in the repository says which** — *`port_vocab` borrows `textwidth` and a pane width; T-138 measured against the same pane and got a different answer.* ***Not taken overnight on purpose***: **a rewrap is visible on every move a player reads, and the question is settled by LOOKING at the description pane in play, which nothing could do at the time.** ***Closes when*** *one width model is proven against the screen, the loser is corrected, and `port_vocab` reports zero.* | `tools/port_vocab.py`, `engineGba/src/move_descriptions.c` — ***DONE AND CLOSED 2026-09-21*** (this commit): ***the pane is 113px and both tools were wrong about it.*** **The description prints into `POKESUM_WIN_TRAINER_MEMO`, which on the moves page is fifteen tiles wide with the text inset seven pixels** — *and `port_vocab` was deriving its ceiling from VANILLA'S WIDEST LINE, 108px, which is a lower bound on a window and not the window.* **Those five pixels are the whole disagreement.** ***Settled on the screen, not by argument***: *driven through the theatre, GOTO's pane reads `a CHECKPOINT alread` with the y cut off at the window edge.* **The rewrap was right and insufficient** — *17 lines in 14 moves fitted no four-line wrap of that wording, TELEPORT worst at 170px* — **so the fourteen were re-said, and the user's own call made them shorter AND better**: ***`them` instead of `the foe`***, *which is 17px cheaper every use and is what this game already believes about the thing across from you.* **Three then needed no shortening at all** — *FATIGUE and PAIR are T-138's own sentences with the pronoun changed.* ***`check_lexicon` caught `they THRASH` on the way***: **1.6 renamed the STATE to THRASHING and `MOVE_THRASH` is BUSY WAIT**, *so the bare verb names a routine.* **356 descriptions, zero over the pane, and `port_vocab` reports nothing.** | T-138, 9.4a |
| **T-120** | **The rest of the shared people sheets, and every trainer portrait** (*tier 3 of T-117*) | **Every shared people sheet left after T-118 and T-119 — the route trainers above all: hiker, fisher, sailor, bug catcher, swimmer, biker and the rest — redrawn once as a fable animal for its CLASS (the species is the role), and all 146 vanilla trainer portraits and 6 back pics likewise, drafted through `daemon/sprite` and cut by `tools/gbachar.py`.** *A class's portrait and its overworld sheet are one species.* ***First batch done 2026-09-15*** (`tools/gentrainers.py`): **the twelve most-used shared sheets — 273 of the 558 objects still on vanilla art — redrawn IN PLACE.** *Nothing is registered and no map changes: a class is one sheet shared by dozens of objects, so overwriting the PNG moves every one of them.* **hiker a badger, fisher a pelican, channeler a bat, woman_2 a ewe, balding_man a vulture, picnicker a hedgehog, bug_catcher a swallow, youngster a mouse, lass a lamb, rocker a skunk, sailor an albatross, old_man_1 an old goat.** ***Two things are fixed and must not change***: *the sheet's PALETTE (one of the four NPC slots, the same on every map — the figure is drawn from what its slot already has) and its FRAME COUNT (the pic table indexes a fixed number; ten here), asserted against the file on disk before anything is written.* **Three failed the fable rule at sixteen pixels and were redrawn**: *a moth's wings read as arms and its antennae as horns (it became a BAT, whose whole silhouette is its ears); a peach dome with a gold bill read as a man with a moustache, the walrus trap again (the vulture's head went dark and small, the bare skin down to a crown patch); and a gold ear on a brown head is a horn, not a spine (the hedgehog lost its ears and gained a crown).* ***The people line moved 22.7% → 27.3%, and the overworld people alone to 50%.*** ***Second batch done 2026-09-15 — the odd formats*** (`tools/genodd.py`): **the four sheets whose frames are not 16×32 — 27 objects.** *`little_girl` a piglet, `little_boy` a bear cub (nine frames, so no raised hand), `tuber_m_water` a duckling and `tuber_f` a cygnet, each in its ring.* **genfolk.py's helpers all assume a 16×32 frame** — *`standing()` pads to 32 rows, `walk()` swings the arms at rows 16–28 and lifts a foot at row 31, `check()` demands 32×16, `sheet()` builds a strip 32 tall* — **so these get their own geometry.** ***A SMALL SPRITE IS A HEAD***: *sixteen rows leave about six for the head and eight for the body, which is why vanilla's children read as heads on feet — and it suits 9.4, where the head's size carries age. The two tubers have no legs at all; the ring and the water take the bottom third.* ***The trap, and it cost three drafts***: **a mirrored half row must reach the CENTRE COLUMN** — *`h16()` pads a half to eight and mirrors it, so a seven-character row leaves column 7 blank and its reflection leaves column 8 blank too, opening a two-pixel transparent seam straight down the middle. Every head read as two lobes and the biker as two riders side by side.* **And `ljust(n)[:n]` pads a short row but SILENTLY CLIPS a long one, which `check()` cannot see because the mirror always yields the right final width** — *so `_fit()` now asserts instead, the same clipping that had been cutting the backs off the town sheets' side heads.* ***The BIKER is deferred, not done***: *10 frames of 32×32 (not twenty), 35 objects — the most-used sheet left. Three drafts in, the boar reads (tan head, dark snout, a tusk either side) but the machine does not: the handlebars stand clear of the body and read as outstretched arms, the head-on wheel reads as a white box, and the silhouette is bulkier than vanilla's; only the side view works. **The next draft takes its proportions from vanilla's own front frame** — a machine no wider than the rider's shoulders, bars angled DOWN into the fork, the front wheel implied by a dark gap rather than a pale rectangle.* ***The people line moved 27.3% → 28.8%, the overworld people to 54%, and every sprite reached 10.0%.*** ***Third batch done 2026-09-15 — the low-use classes*** (`tools/gentrainers.py`): **19 sheets, about 188 objects.** *`gentleman` a lynx, `swimmer_m_water` and `swimmer_m_land` one newt, `man` an ox, `black_belt` a kangaroo, `worker_m` a mule, `crush_girl` a wolverine, `chef` a panda in the hat, `woman_3` a hen, `old_man_2` an old ram with a cane, `swimmer_f_water` and `swimmer_f_land` one axolotl, `camper` a raccoon, `boy` a wallaby, `beauty` a gazelle, `cooltrainer_m` a wolf, `cooltrainer_f` a falcon, `poke_maniac` an opossum, `old_woman` a shrew.* **A class's land and water sheets are ONE animal**, *which is what the swimmers are for.* **A nine-frame sheet takes no raised hand; the count is whatever the file already holds.** ***Two engine facts worth keeping***: *five sheets (`trainer_tower_dude`, `lance`, `bruno`, `agatha`, `gba_kid`) hold only THREE frames while their tables index nine — the table names ids 0,1,2 and repeats them, and every one of those NPCs is `MOVEMENT_TYPE_FACE_DOWN`, so three frames is complete rather than truncated;* **and `sPicTable_OldMan2` borrows `gObjectEventPic_OldWoman` frame 0 as its TENTH entry** — *so the old woman's front frame can render through the old man's WHITE palette rather than her own PINK, latent only because that object never raises a hand.* ***Held back deliberately***: *the named and story characters — `trainer_tower_dude`, `bill` (**HOLT**, 8.2b), `lorelei`, `mr_fuji`, `gym_guy`, `lance`, `bruno`, `agatha`, `celio`, `captain` — because the design already fixes who they are (the Review Board is PHLEGMATIC, CHOLERIC, MELANCHOLIC and SANGUINE), and a named person wants a species for **who they are**, not for a class role; plus `gba_kid`, which needs the three-frame still machinery.* ***The bible was checked before drawing, and it holds***: **8.5's *"nobody else changes"* is a GAME BOY ruling** — *amended in place 2026-09-04 because the port moved the resolution it rested on* — **and 9.4's fable rule (2026-09-06) is the later and more specific one**, *settled by generating four treatments and rejecting the animal-head-on-human-body because it "failed on a bug catcher in shorts", which is a generic class sheet tested directly.* ***8.5's line should be struck as superseded, with the version bump and changelog the process wants — offered, not done.*** ***The people line moved 28.8% → 36.5%, the overworld people to 75% (72 of 96), and every sprite to 11.4%.*** ***Every sheet this project ADDED was scrambled in the ROM, and a playtest screenshot caught it*** (*fixed 2026-09-15; `engine.md` trap 15*). **`spritesheet_rules.mk` names each object-event sheet explicitly and converts it with `-mwidth 2 -mheight 4`, which is what packs a frame's 2×4 tiles together for `overworld_frame()`** — *a strip wider than one frame with no rule is cut in raster order across the whole image, so every frame is assembled from the wrong tiles: the head sheared off, and on the side frame the head down at the feet.* ***Overwriting a vanilla sheet inherits vanilla's rule***, **which is why all three batches of redrawn classes were correct and all 37 sheets we ADDED were wrong from the day they shipped** — *the BENCHMARK guide and nine staff (T-116), the 26 town locals and AL's guest (T-119).* ***Three things hid it***: *`make` compares timestamps and not recipes, so writing the rules rebuilt nothing until the stale `.4bpp` were deleted; four builds exited 0 while the ROM kept the object file it had already linked; and byte-checking a sheet against the ROM cannot see it, because a scrambled sheet verifies perfectly against its own scrambled self.* **The check that works is to DECODE — read the first eight tiles out of the `.4bpp`, assemble them as a 16×32 frame, and compare with frame 0 of the PNG — and it now reads 40 correct, 0 scrambled.** *`genfolk.ensure_rule()` writes the rule whenever a tool saves a new sheet, so the first failure cannot recur.* ***The BIKER took SIX drafts*** (*written 2026-09-15, engine `d84615dcb`* — 35 objects, 10 frames of 32×32, the most-used sheet in the game). **Drafts 1–3 lost the machine**: *handlebars standing clear of the body read as outstretched arms, the head-on wheel as a licence plate, and the whole silhouette was bulkier than the sprite it replaced.* ***Draft 4 fixed that by MEASURING vanilla instead of judging my own drawing*** — **its front frame occupies rows 10–31 only, is at most fourteen columns wide and tapers to two pixels** — *and every earlier draft had been wider than the thing it replaced.* **Draft 5 moved the snout to the centre four columns and put the tusks beside it**, *because a peach dome with a dark band across it reads as a person wearing something — the vulture's failure again.* **Draft 6 gave the crown two rows of ear**: *one pixel of ear reads as nothing, and vanilla's rider carries its whole silhouette in a crest at exactly that height.* ***The rule worth keeping: measure the sprite you are replacing before you redraw it.*** *And a 32×32 sheet must be decoded 4×4 to be checked — decoding it 2×4 reported five of the player's own sprites as scrambled when nothing was wrong.* ***The WATER swimmers were wrong too, and a playtest screenshot caught them standing on the sea***: **a class's land and water sheets are one ANIMAL but not one POSE** — *vanilla's `*_water` sheets are cut at the waterline, head and shoulders with the arms at the surface and nothing below row 28, and I had drawn the land figure into both* (fixed, engine `e9fb1c520`). ***Fourth batch done 2026-09-15 — the named and story characters*** (`tools/gennamed.py`): **eleven sheets.** ***A class gets a species for its ROLE; a named person gets one for WHO THEY ARE***, so these were **read out of the bible and the game's own dialogue rather than invented**: *`lorelei` **PHLEGMATIC** a polar bear (white, FROZEN, "there is no hurry — there never is"), `bruno` **CHOLERIC** a tiger, `agatha` **MELANCHOLIC** a wombat, `lance` **SANGUINE** a cardinal — the seats confirmed from 6's "you fight IV, II, III, I" and from each room's own lines; `bill` **HOLT** a river otter; `mr_fuji` **INIT** a tapir; `celio` a mongoose; the `captain` a cormorant; `gym_guy` a hamster; `trainer_tower_dude` a marmot; `gba_kid` a chipmunk.* ***4.20a names HOLT's species itself*** — **"a holt is specifically an otter's den… an otter is at home in two elements, which is the man who held two frames in one address"** — *so it outranks DOLDRUM's sea otter, chosen later; he is a RIVER otter in the blue slot, on maps that local never stands on.* ***One colour the design mandates could not be given***: **section 6 reserves *sanguine red, choleric yellow, melancholic black, phlegmatic white* to the Board alone, and `bruno` is locked to `npc_white`, which has no yellow** — *its nearest is a sand-gold.* **6804's rule that value carries the humours (*melancholic dark, phlegmatic pale, sanguine and choleric mid*) is satisfied; the hue is not, and that is a compromise rather than a match.** ***The trap this batch taught***: **the raised hand IS the tenth frame** — *`ANIM_RAISE_HAND` indexes it, and every nine-frame sheet here holds nine, so asking for one drew a frame the file had no room for; the assertion against the file on disk caught it before anything was written.* *Five of the eleven are THREE-frame stills, the same ids-0,1,2-repeated case as the Elite Four rooms.* ***The people line moved 36.5% → 40.8%, the overworld people to 86% (83 of 96), and every sprite to 12.1%.*** ***Fifth batch begun 2026-09-15 — the portraits*** (`tools/gbachar.py`, drafted through `daemon/sprite`): **24 cut and in the ROMs.** *The hiker a badger, picnicker a hedgehog, youngster a mouse, bug_catcher a swallow, biker a boar, swimmer_m a newt, lass a lamb, channeler a bat, camper a raccoon, swimmer_f an axolotl, fisherman a pelican, scientist a mole, pokemaniac an opossum, black_belt a kangaroo, crush_girl a wolverine, sailor an albatross, gentleman a lynx, beauty a gazelle, tuber_f a cygnet, rocker a skunk — and the REVIEW BOARD: `elite_four_lorelei` PHLEGMATIC a polar bear, `elite_four_bruno` CHOLERIC a tiger, `elite_four_agatha` MELANCHOLIC a wombat, `elite_four_lance` SANGUINE a cardinal.* ***Every one is the species its overworld sheet already is***, which is this ticket's own rule and meant no new decisions. ***The first drafts came back as TREATMENT C*** — **animal heads on human bodies, with human hands and bare arms** — *which 9.4 rejected because it "failed on a bug catcher in shorts", and the youngster failed exactly that way.* **The prompt now states the three clauses outright** (*paw or claw hands with no human fingers, a tail, a head proportioned to the body; flat cel shading, no gradients*) **and every draft since has passed.** ***And a 4bpp portrait's PNG does not carry its colours***: `trainers.h` takes the picture from `<name>_front_pic.4bpp.lz` and the palette from a SEPARATE `palettes/<name>.gbapal.lz`, *so every job writes its `.pal` too — Scorn once shipped without his and rendered in Giovanni's, where index 5 is magenta.* **Both artefacts are byte-checked in all four ROMs for all 23.** ***The trainer portraits line moved 9% → 25%, and THE PEOPLE REACHED 50.0%*** — *half of every human sprite vanilla shipped is now ours* — **with every sprite at 13.7%.** ***The cut-off, agreed 2026-09-15: a class earns a drawing if THREE OR MORE trainers use it***; below that it keeps vanilla's, because the tail is Hoenn material this game barely shows. **Thirteen more cut on that rule**: *`bird_keeper` an ostrich (33 trainers), `cue_ball` a rhinoceros, `super_nerd` a ring-tailed lemur, `gamer` a jackal, `ruin_maniac` an aardvark, `burglar` a weasel, `juggler` an octopus — and the six PAIR classes, which are TWO figures of one species in a single 64×64: `twins` two quails, `young_couple` two swans (the tuber being a cygnet), `sis_and_bro` two rabbits, `crush_kin` two wolverines (as `crush_girl` already is), `cool_couple` two ibexes, `sr_and_jr` two porcupines.* ***The gamer came back a FOX*** — **russet, brush tail, white tip** — *which 9.4 reserves for the Clears; it took forbidding the fox by name in the prompt to get a black-backed jackal.* **Both figures stay legible in every pair at fifteen colours.** ***37 portraits are now cut: the line moved 9% → 34% (50 of 146), and THE PEOPLE REACHED 55.0%.*** ***And the rule was applied inconsistently in BOTH directions, corrected here***: **ten classes still sit AT OR ABOVE the cut-off and are still vanilla** — *`psychic_f` 5, `pokemon_ranger_m` 5, `pokemon_ranger_f` 5, `painter` 5, `tamer` 4, before drawing, rather than working down a usage list by eye, is what would have caught both.* ***The ten were then drawn (2026-09-15), and EVERY class at or above the cut-off is now done***: beaver, `tamer` a hyena ringmaster, `psychic_f` a jellyfish, `painter` a toucan.* ***Two first picks were species CLASHES and were re-rolled***: **the psychic came back a second octopus — the juggler the chameleon and the octopus by name.* **The portrait line moved 34% → 41% (60 of 146) and the people to 58.8%.** ***Still to come***: **125 portraits are used by a trainer at all; 47 are done; of (33 trainers), `cue_ball` (13), `super_nerd` (9), `gamer` (9), `ruin_maniac` (8), `burglar` (7), `juggler` (6) and the PAIR classes (`twins`, `young_couple`, `sis_and_bro`, `crush_kin`, `cool_couple`, `sr_and_jr`), which are two figures in one 64×64 and need a rule of their own.* **The 6 back pics are untouched.** **`docs/sprites.md`'s people line is the measure** ***The last two people sheets anyone sees, drawn 2026-09-17*** (engine `0c7b1c936`): **of the 13 still vanilla, 11 are never on screen** — *five marked unused, the two surf sheets nothing reads, two named only in a text-colour table, and the Ruby/Sapphire link partners* — **and the two that are:** *`old_man_lying_down`, VIRIDIAN's opening, is the elder iguana's side view turned a quarter clockwise — whole pixels, nothing feet.* ***Found on the way***: **`genodd.py` still said the biker was "not written"; rebuilt in memory all five of its sheets were byte-identical to what ships, so the comment was wrong, not the feature sets.** *Eleven sheets came out of the total because nothing reaches them — `tools/gbaspritecensus.py` now walks the whole chain (an id something SETS → its graphics info → the `.images` pic *Do not work these. They are questions, and the answer belongs in `vision.md`.*

- **THROTTLED's five strays** — SIGNAL owns it six to five (2.7)
- **SUSPENDED's six sleep moves** — CONTEXT owns the concept; retyping the moves is a balance question (2.7)

- **Whether the doctrine has a name, and whether he has one** — *placement settled by T-22: SIX ISLAND's GREEN PATH, somewhere with nobody to check him.* **Both names were left off on purpose and that may be the answer**: naming the doctrine gives the game a thing to hold, and 4.33's whole rule is that it holds nothing (4.33)

## Deferred — decided we want it, decided not yet

*Not blocked and not a question. **Someone looked at the cost and said later.***
An item here needs the reason recorded, or it comes back as a fresh idea in a
month and gets re-argued from nothing.

| | what | why not yet |
|---|---|---|
| **T-18** | **A follower daemon.** One walking behind you, Yellow/HGSS style. *"You bind a daemon and you host it" is an abstraction everywhere except here* — a follower is the one place 1.1's argument would be visible rather than stated | ***The only one of the five ideas with a real RAM cost.*** It wants an object-event slot and sprite tables, and `engine.md` puts the free static space at **1.1 KB of EWRAM and 2.9 KB of IWRAM**. **Measure it before promising it** — every other job on this list is ROM-only and free. *Deferred 2026-09-10, by choice rather than by blocker* ***MEASURED 2026-09-23, and RAM is not the cost.*** **RAM**: *a follower is one of the 16 object-event slots plus a few bytes of state — against 804 B of EWRAM and 2.9 KB of IWRAM free (`gbabudget.py`; this row's 1.1 KB is from September 10).* **Slots**: *16 object events including the player, and objects spawn only near the camera; 16 of 433 maps hold 14 or more templates (KINDLE ROAD 29, ROCK TUNNEL B1F 26), so those are where a crowded screen could refuse one.* **Palette**: *object events own 12 fixed slots (player, four NPC, one special, and a reflection for each); in BLANCHE one of the other four held a field tag and three were free, read live from `sSpritePaletteTags` — so a follower loads its type colour by tag, the way field effects do, with weather and emotes as its only rivals.* ***ART and ROM are the cost.*** **42 overworld daemon sheets exist, all vanilla, and 26 are three still 16×16 frames — no walk.** *A follower needs about nine frames (three facings, two steps each) per daemon:* **at 16×16 that is 1.1 KB each, ~270 KB for the 240 named daemons; at 32×32 it is 4.6 KB each, ~1.1 MB, against ~1.3 MB of ROM left.** *So the promise is a SPRITE job the size of the bestiary, and the size of the sprite decides whether it fits at all.* |

SHARE`**, *and the fifth spanned a line break.* **The stale-name check covered places, moves and species and not ITEMS** — *they live in JSON and needed their own reader, which is why they were skipped* — ***and its filter used `o.isupper()`, which is False for `POKé DOLL`***, so the one item name with an accent stayed invisible on the very run that added items |
